1	// dsp48e2_macc_mreg1: behavioural model of the arch mode dsp48e2_macc_mreg1_mode, which mimics the AMD DSP48E2 slice in the
2	// one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" operation mode (UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12, p46), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
3	// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 1, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY, AMULTSEL = A,
4	// BMULTSEL = B, no pre-adder, no cascade, no pattern detector, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000,
5	// CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
6	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP48E2.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
7	// initial block at lines 1178-1217), read in place and never copied.
8	// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB); M <= A2*B2 if CEM (cleared by RSTM);
9	// P <= f(OPMODE, M, C, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 3 cycles; C and OPMODE to P: 1 cycle
10	// (they are not registered). P wraps at 48 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the eight values below; on
11	// any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X (the mode does not define it).
12	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
13	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
14	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
15	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
16	// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
17	module dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 (
18	    input CLK,
19	    input [26:0] A,
20	    input [17:0] B,
21	    input [47:0] C,
22	    input [8:0] OPMODE,
23	    input RSTA, RSTB, RSTM, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEM, CEP,
24	    output [47:0] P
25	);
26	    reg [26:0] a2 = 0;
27	    reg [17:0] b2 = 0;
28	    reg [47:0] p = 0;
29	    reg signed [44:0] m = 0;                                 // the M register
30	    wire signed [44:0] prod = $signed(a2) * $signed(b2);    // signed 27x18 -> 45, exact
31	    wire [47:0] mx = {{3{m[44]}}, m};
32	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
33	    function [47:0] alu;
34	        input [8:0] opmode;
35	        input [47:0] p;
36	        input [47:0] C;
37	        input [47:0] mx;
38	        case (opmode)
39	            9'b000000101: alu = mx;            // multiply
40	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // multiply-accumulate (Z = P)
41	            9'b000110101: alu = C + mx;        // multiply-add (Z = C)
42	            9'b010110101: alu = p + C + mx;    // multiply-add with P (W = P, Z = C)
43	            9'b110000000: alu = C;             // load C (W = C)
44	            9'b000110000: alu = C;             // load C (Z = C)
45	            9'b010000000: alu = p;             // hold through the ALU (W = P)
46	            9'b000000000: alu = 48'd0;         // zero
47	            default:      alu = {48{1'bx}};    // not a value of this mode
48	        endcase
49	    endfunction
50	    wire [47:0] nxt = alu(OPMODE, p, C, mx);
51	    always @(posedge CLK) begin
52	        if (RSTA) a2 <= 0; else if (CEA2) a2 <= A;
53	        if (RSTB) b2 <= 0; else if (CEB2) b2 <= B;
54	        if (RSTM) m <= 0; else if (CEM) m <= prod;
55	        if (RSTP) p <= 0; else if (CEP) p <= nxt;
56	    end
57	    assign P = p;
58	endmodule

Call 5 · planner · anthropic/claude-opus-5 · ok in 16 · out 48258 · reasoning 43316 · cache read 192220 · cache write 71412 · 682.1 s
prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, conv_layer.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block

This run offered 2 blocks (dsp48e2_macc and dsp48e2_macc_mreg1). Your first approved plan chose dsp48e2_macc_mreg1, described below, and the choice is fixed for the rest of the run: every plan from here on uses dsp48e2_macc_mreg1. The header below has no BLOCK: line; do not write one, even where an earlier plan shown here has it.

  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, the one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" row of UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12 (p46); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p30). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's; the arch file gives the block one internal delay, not one per internal stage.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTM[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEM[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 v1.11 p38: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). AMD's words end with that sentence; the limits that follow are this project's, derived from it. So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. M loads A2*B2 at a clock edge where CEM is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTM. P loads the value OPMODE selects from M, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. P is always registered; C and OPMODE reach P without an input register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 3 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+2); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= M
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + M
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + M
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + M
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB, RSTM and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2, CEM and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTM, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEM and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at C and OPMODE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

Multiplies in the unmodified conv_layer, counted in its packed netlist at seed 1: 84 on 84 DSP tiles, all in two_mult_18x19 mode (1 to a tile). The DSP tile count is the same at each of the five seeds. Multiplies in soft logic, if any, are not in this count.

Each row is one multiply of the design as elaborated from the source. An id names it by its module and by the name the elaborated design gives the multiply, which carries the file and the line; multiplies that come from one line (several written on it, or one inside a generate loop) differ in the number after the last $. An operand's bits are the bits it carries, the zero or sign extension to the width of the expression left out; signed or unsigned is how the multiply reads it. Instances is how many instances of the multiply's module the design holds. Nothing else about a multiply is listed: what its product feeds is in the source.

idsource lineleft operandright operandinstances
qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339assign o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier;i_multiplicand, 16 bits, unsignedi_multiplier, 16 bits, unsigned84
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB, RSTM and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2, CEM and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

Output
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

No rewrite
  • REGIONS: NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or nothing left to try. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the REGIONS line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.

The kept candidate's plan

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
BLOCK: dsp48e2_macc_mreg1
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them, one block each): CLK=clk; A={11'b0,a} (27 b, zero-extended, A[26]=0); B={2'b0,b} (18 b, zero-extended, B[17]=0); C={31'b0,sum_ab_reg} (new 17-bit soft register, see PLAN); OPMODE=(eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 (load C) : 9'b000100101 (multiply-accumulate); RSTA=RSTB=RSTM=RSTP=reset; CEA2=CEB2=CEM=CEP=1'b1; P[31:0] replaces the register add_out_reg (P[47:32] unused). Block registers take over: A2=a_flopped, B2=b_flopped, M=mul_out_reg, P=add_out_reg.

PLAN:

Design read. DWIDTH=16. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846): a_flopped/b_flopped are a/b registered with synchronous reset (2783-2791); qmult forms the 32-bit unsigned product mul_out (2793, 2854); mul_out_reg registers it with the same reset (2795-2801); add_in1/add_in2 select between {16'b0,a_flopped}/{16'b0,b_flopped} and add_out_reg/mul_out_reg on sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add (2807-2808); qadd sums them (2810, 2864) into add_out_reg (2812-2818); mac_out is a saturating down-cast of add_out_reg (2823-2835) and out registers it (2838-2844). So the register update is exactly add_out_reg <= sel ? ({16'b0,a_flopped}+{16'b0,b_flopped}) : (add_out_reg + mul_out_reg), all four registers cleared by the one synchronous reset. Readers checked: a_flopped/b_flopped are read only by the multiply and by the sel muxes; mul_out/mul_out_reg only by the adder path; add_out_reg by the mux and by mac_out; none is a module port. The only module output is out. 84 qmult instances = 84 seq_mac instances (7 matmul_4x4_systolic × 12 live processing_elements, conv_layer.v:1309-1549, 2570-2588, 2648).

Block choice. dsp48e2_macc_mreg1. The design's multiply path has exactly three register stages from a/b to add_out_reg (a_flopped/b_flopped, then mul_out_reg, then add_out_reg), and the accumulate is add_out_reg + mul_out_reg — which is the mreg1 block's A2/B2 → M → P pipeline under OPMODE multiply-accumulate, with the design's single synchronous reset matching RSTA/RSTB/RSTM/RSTP one-for-one. That block absorbs the 16×16 multiply, its two operand registers, the product register, the 32-bit accumulate adder and its result register; what stays in soft logic is the saturating down-cast mac_out, the out register, the OPMODE select, and the small eltwise-add addend path described below. dsp48e2_macc has no M register: it reaches P from A/B in two stages, so matching the design's three stages would require re-adding 32 soft flip-flops per instance ahead of A and B to delay the operands one cycle (and would still absorb nothing more than mreg1 does) — strictly more soft logic for the same arithmetic, so it is not chosen.

Candidate regions. The one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339, is converted. Both operands are 16-bit unsigned: zero-extended they sit at 16 ≤ 26 bits on A and 16 ≤ 17 bits on B, with the top bit of each port 0, so the product is exact on the block.

Blocks added. One block per seq_mac, 84 in total — the same DSP tile count as the unmodified design (84 of the device's 306).

The rewrite, per seq_mac instance.

  1. Delete the registers a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out_reg, add_out_reg, the qmult instance mult_u1, the qadd instance add_u1, and the add_in1/add_in2 wires and their muxes. (Leave the qmult/qadd module definitions in the file; they simply become unused.)
  2. Add one 17-bit soft register with the design's synchronous reset: if (reset) sum_ab_reg <= 0; else sum_ab_reg <= {1'b0,a} + {1'b0,b};. Because a_flopped/b_flopped are a/b delayed one cycle under the same reset, sum_ab_reg equals ({16'b0,a_flopped}+{16'b0,b_flopped})[16:0] on every cycle, including the cycle after any reset (both are 0 there). This recomputes the eltwise-add sum outside the block, since a_flopped/b_flopped live inside it as A2/B2 and are not visible on any block output. Placing the 17-bit add before the flop keeps the C path one soft-flop clock-to-Q away from the block.
  3. Instantiate dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 as in MAPPING. Declare wire [47:0] dsp_p; for P and replace every read of add_out_reg in mac_out (conv_layer.v:2823-2835) with dsp_p[31:0] (keep the existing bit selects and the saturating logic exactly as written).
  4. Keep the out register (2838-2844) unchanged in soft logic.

Why it matches cycle for cycle. By induction, dsp_p[31:0] == add_out_reg at every cycle. Power-up: block registers start at 0 and the original's registers start at 0. Reset edge: RSTP clears P to 0 as the original clears add_out_reg, and RSTA/RSTB/RSTM clear A2/B2/M as the original clears a_flopped/b_flopped/mul_out_reg (resets win over the tied-high clock enables, as the design's resets win over its unconditional loads). Non-reset edge with sel=0: OPMODE 000100101 gives P <= P + M, where M = A2*B2 is the zero-extended 32-bit product (both operands zero-extended and non-negative, so M's 48-bit sign extension is {16'b0, product}); the low 32 bits of the 48-bit sum are the 32-bit wrapped sum the original computes, and only P[31:0] is read. Non-reset edge with sel=1: OPMODE 110000000 gives P <= C = {31'b0, sum_ab_reg}, the original's zero-extended 17-bit sum, which also re-zeroes P[47:32]. sel and C are evaluated at the loading edge, matching the original's combinational muxes. The upper bits P[47:32] can carry accumulator overflow that the original drops, but they are never read and never X, since every OPMODE driven is a legal value.

Register stages, operands to result. Multiply/accumulate path, before: a,b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → mul_out_reg (2) → add_out_reg (3) → out (4). After: a,b → A2/B2 (1) → M (2) → P (3) → soft out (4). Eltwise-add path, before: a,b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → add_out_reg (2) → out (3). After: a,b → sum_ab_reg (1) → P via C (2) → soft out (3). Unchanged in both paths.

DESIGN_CHANGE: in seq_mac's eltwise-add path the addend is a_flopped + b_flopped, operands that the block keeps in A2/B2 where nothing outside can read them; if that path instead took its addend from a value already outside the multiplier pipeline (for example applying the eltwise addend on the C port while add_in1 stays add_out_reg), the 17-bit soft register and adder this rewrite adds per instance would not be needed and the block's C port alone would carry it.

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339

PLAN:

What changed since the kept pass, and why. The kept rewrite is correct and is the result to beat; its measured critical path is entirely the control path into the block's OPMODE pin: the top-level op register (conv_layer.v:847, 1003) → one LUT → 1.835 ns of routing to 84 sinks → the block's 2.140 ns delay → the P setup, 5.412 ns arrival for a 5.389 ns period. OPMODE and C are the only block inputs with no register of their own: a path into them is not cut at the pin and carries the full 2.140 ns through to P. Every other input (A, B, the four resets, the four clock enables) ends at a block register, 0.019 ns setup. So this pass moves the per-cycle mode select off OPMODE and onto RSTP, and makes OPMODE a constant. The same broadcast net then ends at a setup (≈3.2 ns instead of 5.4 ns), and the only thing left driving C is a local flip-flop (0.060 + short route + 2.159 ≈ 2.5 ns). Cost: one extra 17-bit soft register per instance (inj_reg), ≈17 BLEs per seq_mac.

Design read (unchanged from the kept pass). DWIDTH=16. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846): a_flopped/b_flopped are a/b registered under the one synchronous reset (2783-2791); qmult forms the 32-bit unsigned product (2793, 2854); mul_out_reg registers it (2795-2801); the muxes on sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add (2807-2808) and qadd (2810, 2864) make the register update exactly
add_out_reg <= reset ? 0 : (sel ? ({16'b0,a_flopped}+{16'b0,b_flopped}) : (add_out_reg + mul_out_reg)) (2812-2818, 32-bit wrap);
mac_out is the saturating down-cast of add_out_reg (2823-2835) and out registers it (2838-2844). The rewrite, per seq_mac instance. Replace lines 2775-2835 (keep the port list, the out declaration, and the out always block at 2838-2844 as they are) with:

verilog
wire sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

// ({16'b0,a_flopped} + {16'b0,b_flopped}) recomputed outside the block:
// a_flopped/b_flopped live inside it as A2/B2 and are on no block output.
reg [`DWIDTH:0] sum_ab_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) sum_ab_reg <= 0;
  else       sum_ab_reg <= {1'b0, a} + {1'b0, b};
end

// the eltwise-add sum, captured at the edge that performs the eltwise add
// (and zero on every other cycle, so that C adds nothing in MAC mode)
reg [`DWIDTH:0] inj_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset | ~sel) inj_reg <= 0;
  else              inj_reg <= sum_ab_reg;
end

wire [47:0] dsp_p;

dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 mac_dsp_u1 (
  .CLK(clk),
  .A({11'b0, a}),
  .B({2'b0, b}),
  .C({31'b0, inj_reg}),
  .OPMODE(9'b010110101),      // constant: P <= P + C + M
  .RSTA(reset), .RSTB(reset), .RSTM(reset),
  .RSTP(reset | sel),         // the eltwise edge clears the accumulator
  .CEA2(1'b1), .CEB2(1'b1), .CEM(1'b1), .CEP(1'b1),
  .P(dsp_p)
);

// the value the original's add_out_reg holds: dsp_p is all-zero on exactly the
// cycles where inj_reg is non-zero (RSTP cleared P at the previous edge), so the
// OR is a select, not an add.
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = {dsp_p[2*`DWIDTH-1:`DWIDTH+1], (dsp_p[`DWIDTH:0] | inj_reg)};

then keep the down-cast of 2823-2835 verbatim with every add_out_reg replaced by acc, and keep the out register unchanged. qmult/qadd module definitions stay in the file and simply become unused; add_out, add_in1, add_in2, a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out, mul_out_reg, add_out_reg and the mult_u1/add_u1 instances are deleted.

Why it matches cycle for cycle. Write S(t) for the original add_out_reg, M(t) for mul_out_reg, E(t) for sum_ab_reg, I(t) for inj_reg, and sel(t), reset(t) for the values at the edge leaving cycle t.

  • A2/B2/M reproduce a_flopped/b_flopped/mul_out_reg exactly: same loads (CE tied 1), same synchronous clear from the one reset (resets win over the clock enables, as the design's reset wins over its unconditional loads), same power-up value 0.
  • E(t) = ({16'b0,a_flopped(t)}+{16'b0,b_flopped(t)})[16:0] on every cycle, including the cycle after any reset (both are 0 there), because a/b registered under the same reset are a_flopped/b_flopped; the original's 32-bit sum of two zero-extended 16-bit values is exactly {15'b0, E}.
  • Claim A: I(t) ≠ 0 implies dsp_p(t) = 0. I(t) ≠ 0 needs sel(t-1)=1 and reset(t-1)=0, which drove RSTP high at edge t-1, clearing all 48 bits of P. So acc is a clean select of the two and acc(t)[31:0] is well defined.
  • Induction, acc(t) = S(t). After any reset edge both are 0 (RSTP, and the soft registers' reset), and at power-up the block's registers are 0 as the original's are. At an edge with reset=0 and sel=1: the original takes S <= {15'b0,E(t)}; the block takes RSTP high so dsp_p(t+1)=0, and I(t+1)=E(t), so acc(t+1)={15'b0,E(t)}. At an edge with reset=0 and sel=0: I(t+1)=0, and P loads P + C + M = dsp_p(t) + {31'b0,I(t)} + M(t); by Claim A the 48-bit value dsp_p(t)+{31'b0,I(t)} has low 32 bits equal to acc(t)=S(t) (one of the two terms is zero), M's 48-bit form is {16'b0, product} since both operands are zero-extended and non-negative, and carries only propagate upward, so dsp_p(t+1)[31:0] = (S(t)+M(t)) mod 2^32 = S(t+1), and acc(t+1)=dsp_p(t+1)[31:0]. Consecutive eltwise edges are covered by the sel=1 case each time.
  • sel and inj_reg/C are evaluated at the loading edge, as the original's combinational muxes are. OPMODE is a constant legal value, so no edge ever leaves P unknown. P[47:32] may carry accumulator overflow the original drops; it is never read. inj_reg/sum_ab_reg start unknown, but reset clears them and, where inj_reg is unknown before the first reset edge, it only reaches out, which is itself reset — the testbench compares after its 4-cycle reset.

Register stages, operands to result. MAC path, before: a,b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → mul_out_reg (2) → add_out_reg (3) → out (4). After: a,b → A2/B2 (1) → M (2) → P (3) → soft out (4). Eltwise-add path, before: a,b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → add_out_reg (2) → out (3). After: a,b → sum_ab_reg (1) → inj_reg, which is the accumulator's visible value for that cycle while P is held at 0 (2) → soft out (3). Both unchanged; the eltwise value additionally enters P one edge later through C, which restores the block's accumulator state before it is next read.

DESIGN_CHANGE: seq_mac's eltwise-add path adds a_flopped + b_flopped, two values the block keeps in A2/B2 where nothing outside can read them, and it overwrites the accumulator rather than adding to it. If the eltwise addend were instead taken from a value already outside the multiplier pipeline and applied on the accumulator (add_in1 staying add_out_reg), the two 17-bit soft registers this rewrite adds per instance would collapse to none and the block's C port alone would carry it.

1	// dsp48e2_macc_mreg1: behavioural model of the arch mode dsp48e2_macc_mreg1_mode, which mimics the AMD DSP48E2 slice in the
2	// one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" operation mode (UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12, p46), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
3	// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 1, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY, AMULTSEL = A,
4	// BMULTSEL = B, no pre-adder, no cascade, no pattern detector, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000,
5	// CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
6	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP48E2.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
7	// initial block at lines 1178-1217), read in place and never copied.
8	// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB); M <= A2*B2 if CEM (cleared by RSTM);
9	// P <= f(OPMODE, M, C, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 3 cycles; C and OPMODE to P: 1 cycle
10	// (they are not registered). P wraps at 48 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the eight values below; on
11	// any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X (the mode does not define it).
12	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
13	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
14	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
15	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
16	// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
17	module dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 (
18	    input CLK,
19	    input [26:0] A,
20	    input [17:0] B,
21	    input [47:0] C,
22	    input [8:0] OPMODE,
23	    input RSTA, RSTB, RSTM, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEM, CEP,
24	    output [47:0] P
25	);
26	    reg [26:0] a2 = 0;
27	    reg [17:0] b2 = 0;
28	    reg [47:0] p = 0;
29	    reg signed [44:0] m = 0;                                 // the M register
30	    wire signed [44:0] prod = $signed(a2) * $signed(b2);    // signed 27x18 -> 45, exact
31	    wire [47:0] mx = {{3{m[44]}}, m};
32	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
33	    function [47:0] alu;
34	        input [8:0] opmode;
35	        input [47:0] p;
36	        input [47:0] C;
37	        input [47:0] mx;
38	        case (opmode)
39	            9'b000000101: alu = mx;            // multiply
40	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // multiply-accumulate (Z = P)
41	            9'b000110101: alu = C + mx;        // multiply-add (Z = C)
42	            9'b010110101: alu = p + C + mx;    // multiply-add with P (W = P, Z = C)
43	            9'b110000000: alu = C;             // load C (W = C)
44	            9'b000110000: alu = C;             // load C (Z = C)
45	            9'b010000000: alu = p;             // hold through the ALU (W = P)
46	            9'b000000000: alu = 48'd0;         // zero
47	            default:      alu = {48{1'bx}};    // not a value of this mode
48	        endcase
49	    endfunction
50	    wire [47:0] nxt = alu(OPMODE, p, C, mx);
51	    always @(posedge CLK) begin
52	        if (RSTA) a2 <= 0; else if (CEA2) a2 <= A;
53	        if (RSTB) b2 <= 0; else if (CEB2) b2 <= B;
54	        if (RSTM) m <= 0; else if (CEM) m <= prod;
55	        if (RSTP) p <= 0; else if (CEP) p <= nxt;
56	    end
57	    assign P = p;
58	endmodule

Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block

This run offered 2 blocks (dsp48e2_macc and dsp48e2_macc_mreg1). Its first approved plan chose dsp48e2_macc_mreg1, described below, and the choice is fixed for the rest of the run. Check the plan against that block; it must use only dsp48e2_macc_mreg1.

  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, the one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" row of UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12 (p46); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p30). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's; the arch file gives the block one internal delay, not one per internal stage.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTM[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEM[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 v1.11 p38: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). AMD's words end with that sentence; the limits that follow are this project's, derived from it. So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. M loads A2*B2 at a clock edge where CEM is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTM. P loads the value OPMODE selects from M, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. P is always registered; C and OPMODE reach P without an input register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 3 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+2); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= M
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + M
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + M
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + M
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB, RSTM and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2, CEM and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.

The design

The design source is conv_layer.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

Multiplies in the unmodified conv_layer, counted in its packed netlist at seed 1: 84 on 84 DSP tiles, all in two_mult_18x19 mode (1 to a tile). The DSP tile count is the same at each of the five seeds. Multiplies in soft logic, if any, are not in this count.

Each row is one multiply of the design as elaborated from the source. An id names it by its module and by the name the elaborated design gives the multiply, which carries the file and the line; multiplies that come from one line (several written on it, or one inside a generate loop) differ in the number after the last $. An operand's bits are the bits it carries, the zero or sign extension to the width of the expression left out; signed or unsigned is how the multiply reads it. Instances is how many instances of the multiply's module the design holds. Nothing else about a multiply is listed: what its product feeds is in the source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB, RSTM and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2, CEM and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.

PLAN:

Design read. DWIDTH=16. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846): a_flopped/b_flopped are a/b registered with synchronous reset (2783-2791); qmult forms the 32-bit unsigned product mul_out (2793, 2854); mul_out_reg registers it with the same reset (2795-2801); add_in1/add_in2 select between {16'b0,a_flopped}/{16'b0,b_flopped} and add_out_reg/mul_out_reg on sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add (2807-2808); qadd sums them (2810, 2864) into add_out_reg (2812-2818); mac_out is a saturating down-cast of add_out_reg (2823-2835) and out registers it (2838-2844). So the register update is exactly add_out_reg <= sel ? ({16'b0,a_flopped}+{16'b0,b_flopped}) : (add_out_reg + mul_out_reg), all four registers cleared by the one synchronous reset. Readers checked: a_flopped/b_flopped are read only by the multiply and by the sel muxes; mul_out/mul_out_reg only by the adder path; add_out_reg by the mux and by mac_out; none is a module port. The only module output is out. 84 qmult instances = 84 seq_mac instances (7 matmul_4x4_systolic × 12 live processing_elements, conv_layer.v:1309-1549, 2570-2588, 2648).

Block choice. dsp48e2_macc_mreg1. The design's multiply path has exactly three register stages from a/b to add_out_reg (a_flopped/b_flopped, then mul_out_reg, then add_out_reg), and the accumulate is add_out_reg + mul_out_reg — which is the mreg1 block's A2/B2 → M → P pipeline under OPMODE multiply-accumulate, with the design's single synchronous reset matching RSTA/RSTB/RSTM/RSTP one-for-one. That block absorbs the 16×16 multiply, its two operand registers, the product register, the 32-bit accumulate adder and its result register; what stays in soft logic is the saturating down-cast mac_out, the out register, the OPMODE select, and the small eltwise-add addend path described below. dsp48e2_macc has no M register: it reaches P from A/B in two stages, so matching the design's three stages would require re-adding 32 soft flip-flops per instance ahead of A and B to delay the operands one cycle (and would still absorb nothing more than mreg1 does) — strictly more soft logic for the same arithmetic, so it is not chosen.

Candidate regions. The one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339, is converted. Both operands are 16-bit unsigned: zero-extended they sit at 16 ≤ 26 bits on A and 16 ≤ 17 bits on B, with the top bit of each port 0, so the product is exact on the block.

Blocks added. One block per seq_mac, 84 in total — the same DSP tile count as the unmodified design (84 of the device's 306).

The rewrite, per seq_mac instance.

  1. Delete the registers a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out_reg, add_out_reg, the qmult instance mult_u1, the qadd instance add_u1, and the add_in1/add_in2 wires and their muxes. (Leave the qmult/qadd module definitions in the file; they simply become unused.)
  2. Add one 17-bit soft register with the design's synchronous reset: if (reset) sum_ab_reg <= 0; else sum_ab_reg <= {1'b0,a} + {1'b0,b};. Because a_flopped/b_flopped are a/b delayed one cycle under the same reset, sum_ab_reg equals ({16'b0,a_flopped}+{16'b0,b_flopped})[16:0] on every cycle, including the cycle after any reset (both are 0 there). This recomputes the eltwise-add sum outside the block, since a_flopped/b_flopped live inside it as A2/B2 and are not visible on any block output. Placing the 17-bit add before the flop keeps the C path one soft-flop clock-to-Q away from the block.
  3. Instantiate dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 as in MAPPING. Declare wire [47:0] dsp_p; for P and replace every read of add_out_reg in mac_out (conv_layer.v:2823-2835) with dsp_p[31:0] (keep the existing bit selects and the saturating logic exactly as written).
  4. Keep the out register (2838-2844) unchanged in soft logic.

Why it matches cycle for cycle. By induction, dsp_p[31:0] == add_out_reg at every cycle. Power-up: block registers start at 0 and the original's registers start at 0. Reset edge: RSTP clears P to 0 as the original clears add_out_reg, and RSTA/RSTB/RSTM clear A2/B2/M as the original clears a_flopped/b_flopped/mul_out_reg (resets win over the tied-high clock enables, as the design's resets win over its unconditional loads). Non-reset edge with sel=0: OPMODE 000100101 gives P <= P + M, where M = A2*B2 is the zero-extended 32-bit product (both operands zero-extended and non-negative, so M's 48-bit sign extension is {16'b0, product}); the low 32 bits of the 48-bit sum are the 32-bit wrapped sum the original computes, and only P[31:0] is read. Non-reset edge with sel=1: OPMODE 110000000 gives P <= C = {31'b0, sum_ab_reg}, the original's zero-extended 17-bit sum, which also re-zeroes P[47:32]. sel and C are evaluated at the loading edge, matching the original's combinational muxes. The upper bits P[47:32] can carry accumulator overflow that the original drops, but they are never read and never X, since every OPMODE driven is a legal value.

Register stages, operands to result. Multiply/accumulate path, before: a,b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → mul_out_reg (2) → add_out_reg (3) → out (4). After: a,b → A2/B2 (1) → M (2) → P (3) → soft out (4). Eltwise-add path, before: a,b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → add_out_reg (2) → out (3). After: a,b → sum_ab_reg (1) → P via C (2) → soft out (3). Unchanged in both paths.

DESIGN_CHANGE: in seq_mac's eltwise-add path the addend is a_flopped + b_flopped, operands that the block keeps in A2/B2 where nothing outside can read them; if that path instead took its addend from a value already outside the multiplier pipeline (for example applying the eltwise addend on the C port while add_in1 stays add_out_reg), the 17-bit soft register and adder this rewrite adds per instance would not be needed and the block's C port alone would carry it.

The plan

I'll read the design source first.

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them, one block each): CLK=clk; A={11'b0,a} (27 b, zero-extended, A[26]=0); B={2'b0,b} (18 b, zero-extended, B[17]=0); C={31'b0,inj_reg} (new 17-bit soft register, see PLAN); OPMODE=9'b010110101, a constant (P <= P + C + M); RSTA=RSTB=RSTM=reset; RSTP=(reset | (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add)); CEA2=CEB2=CEM=CEP=1'b1; P[47:0] to a new wire [47:0] dsp_p. Block registers take over: A2=a_flopped, B2=b_flopped, M=mul_out_reg; add_out_reg becomes the pair (P, inj_reg), read as acc = {dsp_p[31:17], dsp_p[16:0] | inj_reg}.

PLAN:

What changed since the kept pass, and why. The kept rewrite is correct and is the result to beat; its measured critical path is entirely the control path into the block's OPMODE pin: the top-level op register (conv_layer.v:847, 1003) → one LUT → 1.835 ns of routing to 84 sinks → the block's 2.140 ns delay → the P setup, 5.412 ns arrival for a 5.389 ns period. OPMODE and C are the only block inputs with no register of their own: a path into them is not cut at the pin and carries the full 2.140 ns through to P. Every other input (A, B, the four resets, the four clock enables) ends at a block register, 0.019 ns setup. So this pass moves the per-cycle mode select off OPMODE and onto RSTP, and makes OPMODE a constant. The same broadcast net then ends at a setup (≈3.2 ns instead of 5.4 ns), and the only thing left driving C is a local flip-flop (0.060 + short route + 2.159 ≈ 2.5 ns). Cost: one extra 17-bit soft register per instance (inj_reg), ≈17 BLEs per seq_mac.

Design read (unchanged from the kept pass). DWIDTH=16. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846): a_flopped/b_flopped are a/b registered under the one synchronous reset (2783-2791); qmult forms the 32-bit unsigned product (2793, 2854); mul_out_reg registers it (2795-2801); the muxes on sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add (2807-2808) and qadd (2810, 2864) make the register update exactly
add_out_reg <= reset ? 0 : (sel ? ({16'b0,a_flopped}+{16'b0,b_flopped}) : (add_out_reg + mul_out_reg)) (2812-2818, 32-bit wrap);
mac_out is the saturating down-cast of add_out_reg (2823-2835) and out registers it (2838-2844). Readers checked: a_flopped/b_flopped are read only by the multiply and the sel muxes, mul_out/mul_out_reg only by the adder path, add_out_reg only by the mux and by mac_out; none is a module port, the only output is out. 84 qmult = 84 seq_mac instances (7 matmul_4x4_systolic × 12 live processing_elements, conv_layer.v:1309-1549, 2570-2588, 2648). Note the module boundary is fixed: the equivalence testbench instantiates seq_mac with exactly these ports, so no port may be added and nothing may be hoisted into processing_element.

Candidate regions. The one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339, is converted (84 instances). Both operands are 16-bit unsigned: zero-extended they sit at 16 ≤ 26 bits on A and 16 ≤ 17 bits on B, top bit of each port 0, so the product is exact. Nothing is left unconverted.

Blocks added. One dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 per seq_mac, 84 in total — the same DSP tile count as the unmodified design (84 of 306).

The rewrite, per seq_mac instance. Replace lines 2775-2835 (keep the port list, the out declaration, and the out always block at 2838-2844 as they are) with:

verilog
wire sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

// ({16'b0,a_flopped} + {16'b0,b_flopped}) recomputed outside the block:
// a_flopped/b_flopped live inside it as A2/B2 and are on no block output.
reg [`DWIDTH:0] sum_ab_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) sum_ab_reg <= 0;
  else       sum_ab_reg <= {1'b0, a} + {1'b0, b};
end

// the eltwise-add sum, captured at the edge that performs the eltwise add
// (and zero on every other cycle, so that C adds nothing in MAC mode)
reg [`DWIDTH:0] inj_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset | ~sel) inj_reg <= 0;
  else              inj_reg <= sum_ab_reg;
end

wire [47:0] dsp_p;

dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 mac_dsp_u1 (
  .CLK(clk),
  .A({11'b0, a}),
  .B({2'b0, b}),
  .C({31'b0, inj_reg}),
  .OPMODE(9'b010110101),      // constant: P <= P + C + M
  .RSTA(reset), .RSTB(reset), .RSTM(reset),
  .RSTP(reset | sel),         // the eltwise edge clears the accumulator
  .CEA2(1'b1), .CEB2(1'b1), .CEM(1'b1), .CEP(1'b1),
  .P(dsp_p)
);

// the value the original's add_out_reg holds: dsp_p is all-zero on exactly the
// cycles where inj_reg is non-zero (RSTP cleared P at the previous edge), so the
// OR is a select, not an add.
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = {dsp_p[2*`DWIDTH-1:`DWIDTH+1], (dsp_p[`DWIDTH:0] | inj_reg)};

then keep the down-cast of 2823-2835 verbatim with every add_out_reg replaced by acc, and keep the out register unchanged. qmult/qadd module definitions stay in the file and simply become unused; add_out, add_in1, add_in2, a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out, mul_out_reg, add_out_reg and the mult_u1/add_u1 instances are deleted.

Why it matches cycle for cycle. Write S(t) for the original add_out_reg, M(t) for mul_out_reg, E(t) for sum_ab_reg, I(t) for inj_reg, and sel(t), reset(t) for the values at the edge leaving cycle t.

  • A2/B2/M reproduce a_flopped/b_flopped/mul_out_reg exactly: same loads (CE tied 1), same synchronous clear from the one reset (resets win over the clock enables, as the design's reset wins over its unconditional loads), same power-up value 0.
  • E(t) = ({16'b0,a_flopped(t)}+{16'b0,b_flopped(t)})[16:0] on every cycle, including the cycle after any reset (both are 0 there), because a/b registered under the same reset are a_flopped/b_flopped; the original's 32-bit sum of two zero-extended 16-bit values is exactly {15'b0, E}.
  • Claim A: I(t) ≠ 0 implies dsp_p(t) = 0. I(t) ≠ 0 needs sel(t-1)=1 and reset(t-1)=0, which drove RSTP high at edge t-1, clearing all 48 bits of P. So acc is a clean select of the two and acc(t)[31:0] is well defined.
  • Induction, acc(t) = S(t). After any reset edge both are 0 (RSTP, and the soft registers' reset), and at power-up the block's registers are 0 as the original's are. At an edge with reset=0 and sel=1: the original takes S <= {15'b0,E(t)}; the block takes RSTP high so dsp_p(t+1)=0, and I(t+1)=E(t), so acc(t+1)={15'b0,E(t)}. At an edge with reset=0 and sel=0: I(t+1)=0, and P loads P + C + M = dsp_p(t) + {31'b0,I(t)} + M(t); by Claim A the 48-bit value dsp_p(t)+{31'b0,I(t)} has low 32 bits equal to acc(t)=S(t) (one of the two terms is zero), M's 48-bit form is {16'b0, product} since both operands are zero-extended and non-negative, and carries only propagate upward, so dsp_p(t+1)[31:0] = (S(t)+M(t)) mod 2^32 = S(t+1), and acc(t+1)=dsp_p(t+1)[31:0]. Consecutive eltwise edges are covered by the sel=1 case each time.
  • sel and inj_reg/C are evaluated at the loading edge, as the original's combinational muxes are. OPMODE is a constant legal value, so no edge ever leaves P unknown. P[47:32] may carry accumulator overflow the original drops; it is never read. inj_reg/sum_ab_reg start unknown, but reset clears them and, where inj_reg is unknown before the first reset edge, it only reaches out, which is itself reset — the testbench compares after its 4-cycle reset.

Register stages, operands to result. MAC path, before: a,b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → mul_out_reg (2) → add_out_reg (3) → out (4). After: a,b → A2/B2 (1) → M (2) → P (3) → soft out (4). Eltwise-add path, before: a,b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → add_out_reg (2) → out (3). After: a,b → sum_ab_reg (1) → inj_reg, which is the accumulator's visible value for that cycle while P is held at 0 (2) → soft out (3). Both unchanged; the eltwise value additionally enters P one edge later through C, which restores the block's accumulator state before it is next read.

DESIGN_CHANGE: seq_mac's eltwise-add path adds a_flopped + b_flopped, two values the block keeps in A2/B2 where nothing outside can read them, and it overwrites the accumulator rather than adding to it. If the eltwise addend were instead taken from a value already outside the multiplier pipeline and applied on the accumulator (add_in1 staying add_out_reg), the two 17-bit soft registers this rewrite adds per instance would collapse to none and the block's C port alone would carry it.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of seq_mac on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp48e2_macc_mreg1.v) are in sim_reference/.

Checks performed against conv_layer.v:

  • Design read. seq_mac at 2765–2846 matches the plan's description: a_flopped/b_flopped at 2783–2791, qmult at 2793 (product line 2854), mul_out_reg at 2795–2801, the sel muxes at 2807–2808, qadd at 2810/2864, add_out_reg at 2812–2818, the saturating down-cast at 2823–2835, out at 2838–2844. All four internal registers use the single synchronous reset, unconditional loads otherwise. The register update really is add_out_reg <= reset ? 0 : (sel ? ({16'b0,a_flopped}+{16'b0,b_flopped}) : (add_out_reg+mul_out_reg)).
  • Visibility. a_flopped/b_flopped are read only at 2793, 2807, 2808; mul_out/mul_out_reg only at 2793/2808; add_out_reg only at 2807 and 2823–2835. None is a module port; the only output is out (2768). So absorbing them into A2/B2/M/P is legal, and the eltwise addend must be recomputed outside — which sum_ab_reg does, equal to ({16'b0,a_flopped}+{16'b0,b_flopped})[16:0] every cycle including the cycle after any reset.
  • Block usage. Only dsp48e2_macc_mreg1; all 13 inputs connected; OPMODE is the legal constant 010110101 (P <= P+C+M, per the contract and sim_reference/dsp48e2_macc_mreg1.v:42), so no edge can leave P as X. Widths fit: A = 27 b with A[26]=0, B = 18 b with B[17]=0 (16-bit unsigned operands, within the 26/17 limits), C = 48 b.
  • Behavior. Claim A holds: inj_reg(t) ≠ 0 requires reset=0, sel=1 at edge t−1, which drove RSTP = reset|sel high and cleared all 48 bits of P, so acc = {dsp_p[31:17], dsp_p[16:0]|inj_reg} is a select, never a spurious add. The sel=1 edge gives acc = {15'b0,E} = the original's zero-extended 17-bit sum; the sel=0 edge gives P+C+M whose low 32 bits are (S+M) mod 2^32 (M is {16'b0,product}, carries propagate only upward), matching the original's 32-bit wrap. Resets win over the tied-high enables, matching the design's reset-dominant loads; back-to-back eltwise edges and reset-during-eltwise both resolve correctly. P[47:32] is never read.
  • Start-up/X. Added soft registers start unknown but are cleared by reset; the testbench (sim_reference/tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:154-156) holds reset for 4 cycles and only then sets cmp, so no X reaches a compared cycle. The rewrite does not rely on their initial value.
  • Required items. Candidate regions (the single one, converted, none left), block count (84, one per seq_mac, matching the 84 instances), and register stages before/after for both the MAC path (4 stages) and the eltwise path (3 stages) are all stated and correct. No ASSUMES: line is needed, and none is claimed.

1	// dsp48e2_macc_mreg1: behavioural model of the arch mode dsp48e2_macc_mreg1_mode, which mimics the AMD DSP48E2 slice in the
2	// one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" operation mode (UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12, p46), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
3	// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 1, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY, AMULTSEL = A,
4	// BMULTSEL = B, no pre-adder, no cascade, no pattern detector, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000,
5	// CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
6	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP48E2.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
7	// initial block at lines 1178-1217), read in place and never copied.
8	// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB); M <= A2*B2 if CEM (cleared by RSTM);
9	// P <= f(OPMODE, M, C, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 3 cycles; C and OPMODE to P: 1 cycle
10	// (they are not registered). P wraps at 48 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the eight values below; on
11	// any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X (the mode does not define it).
12	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
13	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
14	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
15	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
16	// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
17	module dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 (

20	    input [17:0] B,
21	    input [47:0] C,
22	    input [8:0] OPMODE,
23	    input RSTA, RSTB, RSTM, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEM, CEP,

30	    wire signed [44:0] prod = $signed(a2) * $signed(b2);    // signed 27x18 -> 45, exact
31	    wire [47:0] mx = {{3{m[44]}}, m};
32	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
33	    function [47:0] alu;

40	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // multiply-accumulate (Z = P)
41	            9'b000110101: alu = C + mx;        // multiply-add (Z = C)
42	            9'b010110101: alu = p + C + mx;    // multiply-add with P (W = P, Z = C)

54	        if (RSTM) m <= 0; else if (CEM) m <= prod;
55	        if (RSTP) p <= 0; else if (CEP) p <= nxt;

Task

Edit conv_layer.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block

This run offered 2 blocks (dsp48e2_macc and dsp48e2_macc_mreg1). Its first approved plan chose dsp48e2_macc_mreg1, described below, and the choice is fixed for the rest of the run. Instantiate only dsp48e2_macc_mreg1: an edit that instantiates another offered block is refused.

  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, the one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" row of UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12 (p46); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p30). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's; the arch file gives the block one internal delay, not one per internal stage.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTM[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEM[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 v1.11 p38: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). AMD's words end with that sentence; the limits that follow are this project's, derived from it. So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. M loads A2*B2 at a clock edge where CEM is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTM. P loads the value OPMODE selects from M, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. P is always registered; C and OPMODE reach P without an input register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 3 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+2); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= M
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + M
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + M
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + M
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB, RSTM and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2, CEM and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTM, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEM and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at C and OPMODE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Multiply in the approved plan: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339 (module qmult).
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB, RSTM and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2, CEM and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the approved plan calls for.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh conv_layer.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them, one block each): CLK=clk; A={11'b0,a} (27 b, zero-extended, A[26]=0); B={2'b0,b} (18 b, zero-extended, B[17]=0); C={31'b0,inj_reg} (new 17-bit soft register, see PLAN); OPMODE=9'b010110101, a constant (P <= P + C + M); RSTA=RSTB=RSTM=reset; RSTP=(reset | (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add)); CEA2=CEB2=CEM=CEP=1'b1; P[47:0] to a new wire [47:0] dsp_p. Block registers take over: A2=a_flopped, B2=b_flopped, M=mul_out_reg; add_out_reg becomes the pair (P, inj_reg), read as acc = {dsp_p[31:17], dsp_p[16:0] | inj_reg}.

PLAN:

What changed since the kept pass, and why. The kept rewrite is correct and is the result to beat; its measured critical path is entirely the control path into the block's OPMODE pin: the top-level op register (conv_layer.v:847, 1003) → one LUT → 1.835 ns of routing to 84 sinks → the block's 2.140 ns delay → the P setup, 5.412 ns arrival for a 5.389 ns period. OPMODE and C are the only block inputs with no register of their own: a path into them is not cut at the pin and carries the full 2.140 ns through to P. Every other input (A, B, the four resets, the four clock enables) ends at a block register, 0.019 ns setup. So this pass moves the per-cycle mode select off OPMODE and onto RSTP, and makes OPMODE a constant. The same broadcast net then ends at a setup (≈3.2 ns instead of 5.4 ns), and the only thing left driving C is a local flip-flop (0.060 + short route + 2.159 ≈ 2.5 ns). Cost: one extra 17-bit soft register per instance (inj_reg), ≈17 BLEs per seq_mac.

Design read (unchanged from the kept pass). DWIDTH=16. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846): a_flopped/b_flopped are a/b registered under the one synchronous reset (2783-2791); qmult forms the 32-bit unsigned product (2793, 2854); mul_out_reg registers it (2795-2801); the muxes on sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add (2807-2808) and qadd (2810, 2864) make the register update exactly
add_out_reg <= reset ? 0 : (sel ? ({16'b0,a_flopped}+{16'b0,b_flopped}) : (add_out_reg + mul_out_reg)) (2812-2818, 32-bit wrap);
mac_out is the saturating down-cast of add_out_reg (2823-2835) and out registers it (2838-2844). Readers checked: a_flopped/b_flopped are read only by the multiply and the sel muxes, mul_out/mul_out_reg only by the adder path, add_out_reg only by the mux and by mac_out; none is a module port, the only output is out. 84 qmult = 84 seq_mac instances (7 matmul_4x4_systolic × 12 live processing_elements, conv_layer.v:1309-1549, 2570-2588, 2648). Note the module boundary is fixed: the equivalence testbench instantiates seq_mac with exactly these ports, so no port may be added and nothing may be hoisted into processing_element.

Candidate regions. The one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339, is converted (84 instances). Both operands are 16-bit unsigned: zero-extended they sit at 16 ≤ 26 bits on A and 16 ≤ 17 bits on B, top bit of each port 0, so the product is exact. Nothing is left unconverted.

Blocks added. One dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 per seq_mac, 84 in total — the same DSP tile count as the unmodified design (84 of 306).

The rewrite, per seq_mac instance. Replace lines 2775-2835 (keep the port list, the out declaration, and the out always block at 2838-2844 as they are) with:

verilog
wire sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

// ({16'b0,a_flopped} + {16'b0,b_flopped}) recomputed outside the block:
// a_flopped/b_flopped live inside it as A2/B2 and are on no block output.
reg [`DWIDTH:0] sum_ab_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) sum_ab_reg <= 0;
  else       sum_ab_reg <= {1'b0, a} + {1'b0, b};
end

// the eltwise-add sum, captured at the edge that performs the eltwise add
// (and zero on every other cycle, so that C adds nothing in MAC mode)
reg [`DWIDTH:0] inj_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset | ~sel) inj_reg <= 0;
  else              inj_reg <= sum_ab_reg;
end

wire [47:0] dsp_p;

dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 mac_dsp_u1 (
  .CLK(clk),
  .A({11'b0, a}),
  .B({2'b0, b}),
  .C({31'b0, inj_reg}),
  .OPMODE(9'b010110101),      // constant: P <= P + C + M
  .RSTA(reset), .RSTB(reset), .RSTM(reset),
  .RSTP(reset | sel),         // the eltwise edge clears the accumulator
  .CEA2(1'b1), .CEB2(1'b1), .CEM(1'b1), .CEP(1'b1),
  .P(dsp_p)
);

// the value the original's add_out_reg holds: dsp_p is all-zero on exactly the
// cycles where inj_reg is non-zero (RSTP cleared P at the previous edge), so the
// OR is a select, not an add.
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = {dsp_p[2*`DWIDTH-1:`DWIDTH+1], (dsp_p[`DWIDTH:0] | inj_reg)};

then keep the down-cast of 2823-2835 verbatim with every add_out_reg replaced by acc, and keep the out register unchanged. qmult/qadd module definitions stay in the file and simply become unused; add_out, add_in1, add_in2, a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out, mul_out_reg, add_out_reg and the mult_u1/add_u1 instances are deleted.

Why it matches cycle for cycle. Write S(t) for the original add_out_reg, M(t) for mul_out_reg, E(t) for sum_ab_reg, I(t) for inj_reg, and sel(t), reset(t) for the values at the edge leaving cycle t.

  • A2/B2/M reproduce a_flopped/b_flopped/mul_out_reg exactly: same loads (CE tied 1), same synchronous clear from the one reset (resets win over the clock enables, as the design's reset wins over its unconditional loads), same power-up value 0.
  • E(t) = ({16'b0,a_flopped(t)}+{16'b0,b_flopped(t)})[16:0] on every cycle, including the cycle after any reset (both are 0 there), because a/b registered under the same reset are a_flopped/b_flopped; the original's 32-bit sum of two zero-extended 16-bit values is exactly {15'b0, E}.
  • Claim A: I(t) ≠ 0 implies dsp_p(t) = 0. I(t) ≠ 0 needs sel(t-1)=1 and reset(t-1)=0, which drove RSTP high at edge t-1, clearing all 48 bits of P. So acc is a clean select of the two and acc(t)[31:0] is well defined.
  • Induction, acc(t) = S(t). After any reset edge both are 0 (RSTP, and the soft registers' reset), and at power-up the block's registers are 0 as the original's are. At an edge with reset=0 and sel=1: the original takes S <= {15'b0,E(t)}; the block takes RSTP high so dsp_p(t+1)=0, and I(t+1)=E(t), so acc(t+1)={15'b0,E(t)}. At an edge with reset=0 and sel=0: I(t+1)=0, and P loads P + C + M = dsp_p(t) + {31'b0,I(t)} + M(t); by Claim A the 48-bit value dsp_p(t)+{31'b0,I(t)} has low 32 bits equal to acc(t)=S(t) (one of the two terms is zero), M's 48-bit form is {16'b0, product} since both operands are zero-extended and non-negative, and carries only propagate upward, so dsp_p(t+1)[31:0] = (S(t)+M(t)) mod 2^32 = S(t+1), and acc(t+1)=dsp_p(t+1)[31:0]. Consecutive eltwise edges are covered by the sel=1 case each time.
  • sel and inj_reg/C are evaluated at the loading edge, as the original's combinational muxes are. OPMODE is a constant legal value, so no edge ever leaves P unknown. P[47:32] may carry accumulator overflow the original drops; it is never read. inj_reg/sum_ab_reg start unknown, but reset clears them and, where inj_reg is unknown before the first reset edge, it only reaches out, which is itself reset — the testbench compares after its 4-cycle reset.

Register stages, operands to result. MAC path, before: a,b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → mul_out_reg (2) → add_out_reg (3) → out (4). After: a,b → A2/B2 (1) → M (2) → P (3) → soft out (4). Eltwise-add path, before: a,b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → add_out_reg (2) → out (3). After: a,b → sum_ab_reg (1) → inj_reg, which is the accumulator's visible value for that cycle while P is held at 0 (2) → soft out (3). Both unchanged; the eltwise value additionally enters P one edge later through C, which restores the block's accumulator state before it is next read.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of seq_mac on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp48e2_macc_mreg1.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

14:`define DWIDTH 16
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true
