The plan

I'll read the design source before planning.

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them, one block each): CLK=clk; A={11'b0,a} (27 b, zero-extended, A[26]=0); B={2'b0,b} (18 b, zero-extended, B[17]=0); C={31'b0,inj_reg} (17-bit soft register, see PLAN); OPMODE=9'b010110101, a constant (P <= P + C + M); RSTA=RSTB=RSTM=reset; RSTP=(reset | (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add)); CEA2=CEB2=CEM=CEP=1'b1; P[47:0] to a new wire [47:0] dsp_p. Block registers take over: A2/B2 hold a/b for the multiply, M=mul_out_reg; add_out_reg becomes the pair (P, inj_reg), read as acc = {dsp_p[31:17], dsp_p[16:0] | inj_reg}. a_flopped/b_flopped stay in soft logic as the first stage of the eltwise-add path.

PLAN:

What changes from the kept pass (revision 1), and why. Revision 1 is correct and is the result to beat. Its measured critical path at seed 1 is the register it added: launch flop → three LUT levels of the array's a/b feed (0.660 + 0.452 + 1.494 ns of routing) → $add~1174^ADD~118, the 17-bit {1'b0,a}+{1'b0,b} adder (1.000 wire in, 0.422 carry chain, 0.868 wire out) → the reset-AND LUT → $sdff~1177^Q~15.D, 5.627 ns arrival for a 5.603 ns period. That is sum_ab_reg bit 15. In the unmodified design the same upstream chain ended at a flip-flop setup (a_flopped), about 3.2 ns; revision 1 appended ~2.4 ns of adder and routing to it, and that single path now sets the period at every seed (seed 1 5.603, median 5.342).

This pass moves the adder off the a/b input path: the first stage of the eltwise-add path becomes the design's own a_flopped/b_flopped (16 + 16 soft flip-flops, D driven straight from a/b), and the 17-bit add runs flip-flop-to-flip-flop into inj_reg. Every block input then ends either at a block register (0.019 ns setup: A, B, the four resets, the four clock enables) or, for C, at one local flip-flop ahead of the block's 2.159 ns (inj_reg → C). OPMODE stays a constant, so the high-fanout op broadcast never sees the block's 2.140 ns delay — that was the first pass's 5.4 ns path and must not come back.

Cost, stated plainly: 15 extra soft flip-flops per instance (49 instead of 34), about +1260 BLEs over the design, which I estimate at roughly +7% used area. Break-even against revision 1 is therefore a median period near 5.0 ns; every path I can account for inside seq_mac after this change is at most ~3.9 ns (the inj_reg → C → P path), so the gamble is on what the untouched rest of conv_layer holds, which revision 1's median bounds at ≤5.34 ns.

Design read. DWIDTH=16. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846): a_flopped/b_flopped are a/b registered under the one synchronous reset (2783-2791); qmult forms the 32-bit unsigned product (2793, 2854); mul_out_reg registers it (2795-2801); the muxes on sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add (2807-2808) and qadd (2810, 2864) make the accumulator update exactly
add_out_reg <= reset ? 0 : (sel ? ({16'b0,a_flopped}+{16'b0,b_flopped}) : (add_out_reg + mul_out_reg)) (2812-2818, 32-bit wrap);
mac_out is the saturating down-cast of add_out_reg (2823-2835) and out registers it (2838-2844). Readers checked: a_flopped/b_flopped are read only by the multiply and the sel muxes, mul_out/mul_out_reg only by the adder path, add_out_reg only by the mux and by mac_out; none is a module port, the only output is out. 84 qmult = 84 seq_mac instances (7 matmul_4x4_systolic × 12 live processing_elements, conv_layer.v:1309-1549, 2570-2588, 2648). The module boundary is fixed: tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:42-43 instantiates seq_mac with exactly these ports and drives a, b, eltwise_mode, eltwise_add, reset randomly, so no port may be added, nothing may be hoisted into processing_element, and no assumption about how op/ready_for_eltwise_op sequence the module may be used.

Candidate regions. The one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339, is converted (84 instances). Both operands are 16-bit unsigned: zero-extended they sit at 16 ≤ 26 bits on A and 16 ≤ 17 bits on B, so the top bit of each multiplier operand is 0 and the product is exact. Nothing is left unconverted.

Blocks added. One dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 per seq_mac, 84 in total — the same DSP tile count as the unmodified design (84 of 306).

The rewrite, per seq_mac instance. Replace lines 2775-2835 (keep the port list and the reg [DWIDTH-1:0] out; declaration at 2774, and keep the out` always block at 2838-2844 exactly as it is) with:

verilog
wire sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

// stage 1 of the eltwise-add path: the design's own a_flopped/b_flopped, kept
// in soft logic because A2/B2 inside the block hold the same values but are on
// no block output. D comes straight from a/b, so nothing is added to the
// late-arriving a/b input path.
reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] a_flopped;
reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] b_flopped;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    a_flopped <= 0;
    b_flopped <= 0;
  end else begin
    a_flopped <= a;
    b_flopped <= b;
  end
end

// the 17-bit eltwise sum, now formed register-to-register
wire [`DWIDTH:0] sum_ab = {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};

// stage 2: the eltwise-add result, zero on every other cycle so that C adds
// nothing in MAC mode
reg [`DWIDTH:0] inj_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset | ~sel) inj_reg <= 0;
  else              inj_reg <= sum_ab;
end

wire [47:0] dsp_p;

dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 mac_dsp_u1 (
  .CLK(clk),
  .A({11'b0, a}),
  .B({2'b0, b}),
  .C({31'b0, inj_reg}),
  .OPMODE(9'b010110101),      // constant: P <= P + C + M
  .RSTA(reset), .RSTB(reset), .RSTM(reset),
  .RSTP(reset | sel),         // the eltwise edge clears the accumulator
  .CEA2(1'b1), .CEB2(1'b1), .CEM(1'b1), .CEP(1'b1),
  .P(dsp_p)
);

// the value the original's add_out_reg holds: dsp_p is all-zero on exactly the
// cycles where inj_reg may be non-zero (RSTP cleared P at the previous edge),
// so the OR is a select, not an add.
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = {dsp_p[2*`DWIDTH-1:`DWIDTH+1], (dsp_p[`DWIDTH:0] | inj_reg)};

then keep the down-cast of 2823-2835 verbatim with every add_out_reg replaced by acc, and keep the out register unchanged. qmult/qadd module definitions stay in the file and simply become unused; add_out, add_in1, add_in2, mul_out, mul_out_reg, add_out_reg and the mult_u1/add_u1 instances are deleted. a_flopped/b_flopped and their always block are kept exactly as the original writes them.

Why it matches cycle for cycle. Write S(t) for the original add_out_reg, M₀(t) for mul_out_reg, I(t) for inj_reg, and sel(t), reset(t) for the values present at the edge leaving cycle t.

  • A2/B2/M reproduce a_flopped/b_flopped/mul_out_reg exactly: same loads (clock enables tied 1), same synchronous clear from the one reset (a reset wins over its register's clock enable, as the design's reset wins over its unconditional loads), same power-up value 0.
  • a_flopped/b_flopped in soft logic are the original's registers verbatim, so sum_ab(t) = ({16'b0,a_flopped(t)}+{16'b0,b_flopped(t)})[16:0] on every cycle, including the cycle after any reset (both operands 0 there). The original's 32-bit sum of two zero-extended 16-bit values is exactly {15'b0, sum_ab}.
  • Claim A: I(t) ≠ 0 implies dsp_p(t) = 0. I(t) ≠ 0 needs sel(t-1)=1 and reset(t-1)=0, which drove RSTP high at edge t-1, clearing all 48 bits of P. So acc is a clean select of the two and acc(t)[31:0] is well defined.
  • Induction, acc(t) = S(t). After any reset edge both are 0 (RSTP, and inj_reg's reset), and at power-up the block's registers are 0 as the original's are. At an edge with reset=0 and sel=1: the original takes S <= {15'b0, sum_ab(t)}; the block takes RSTP high so dsp_p(t+1)=0, and I(t+1)=sum_ab(t), so acc(t+1)={15'b0, sum_ab(t)}. At an edge with reset=0 and sel=0: I(t+1)=0, and P loads P + C + M = dsp_p(t) + {31'b0,I(t)} + M(t); by Claim A one of the first two terms is zero, so that 48-bit sum has low 32 bits equal to acc(t)=S(t); M's 48-bit form is {16'b0, product} since both operands are zero-extended and non-negative (the model sign-extends m, whose top bit is 0); carries only propagate upward, so dsp_p(t+1)[31:0] = (S(t)+M₀(t)) mod 2^32 = S(t+1), and acc(t+1)=dsp_p(t+1)[31:0]. Consecutive eltwise edges are covered by the sel=1 case each time, and an eltwise edge followed by MAC edges is covered by the C injection, which puts the exact 17-bit sum back into the accumulator.
  • sel and inj_reg/C are evaluated at the loading edge, as the original's combinational muxes are. OPMODE is a constant legal value, so no edge ever leaves P unknown. P[47:32] may carry accumulator overflow the original drops; it is never read. inj_reg starts unknown, but reset clears it and, before the first reset edge, it reaches only out, which is itself reset; the testbench compares from the first cycle after its 4-cycle reset (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:154-156).

Register stages, operands to result. MAC path, before: a,b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → mul_out_reg (2) → add_out_reg (3) → out (4). After: a,b → A2/B2 (1) → M (2) → P (3) → soft out (4). Eltwise-add path, before: a,b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → add_out_reg (2) → out (3). After: a,b → soft a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → inj_reg, which is the accumulator's visible value for that cycle while P is held at 0 (2) → soft out (3). Both unchanged; the eltwise value additionally enters P one edge later through C, which restores the block's accumulator state before it is next read. Relative to revision 1 the stage count is identical; only the adder's position moved from ahead of stage 1 to between stages 1 and 2.

DESIGN_CHANGE: seq_mac's eltwise-add path adds a_flopped + b_flopped, two values the block keeps in A2/B2 where nothing outside can read them, and it overwrites the accumulator rather than adding to it. Either of two source changes would delete all 49 soft flip-flops this rewrite adds per instance: taking the eltwise addend from a value already outside the multiplier pipeline and applying it on the accumulator (add_in1 staying add_out_reg), so that C alone carries it; or adding the current inputs a + b rather than the flopped pair, which would cut the eltwise path to one stage and let C be driven from a single register.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of seq_mac on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp48e2_macc_mreg1.v) are in sim_reference/.

Checks performed against conv_layer.v, sim_reference/dsp48e2_macc_mreg1.v and sim_reference/tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:

  • Design claims. DWIDTH=16 (line 14). seq_mac at 2765–2846 matches the plan's read: a_flopped/b_flopped registered from a/b under the single synchronous reset (2783–2791); qmult is an unsigned 16×16→32 product (2793, 2849–2854); mul_out_reg (2795–2801); the eltwise_mode & eltwise_add muxes (2807–2808) with qadd (2810, 2864) give exactly the stated add_out_reg update (2812–2818); saturating down-cast 2823–2835; out 2838–2844. Reader check confirmed by grep: a_flopped/b_flopped appear only at 2793 and 2807–2808, mul_out_reg only at 2808, add_out_reg only at 2807 and in the down-cast — none is a port, out is the only output. 84 instances = 7 matmul_4x4_systolic (1309–1549) × 12 live processing_elements (2570–2588, three commented out per row) × one seq_mac (2648). Testbench citations 42–43 and 154–156 are accurate; ports are fixed, so keeping the work inside seq_mac is required.
  • Contract use. Only dsp48e2_macc_mreg1; every input port driven; A={11'b0,a} (27 b, A[26]=0), B={2'b0,b} (18 b, B[17]=0) — 16≤26 and 16≤17, so the unsigned product is exact and mx is non-negative; C={31'b0,inj_reg} (48 b); OPMODE 9'b010110101 is a listed legal value, constant, so no edge leaves P as X; CEs tied 1, resets driven; RSTA/RSTB/RSTM=reset reproduce the design's unconditional-load-with-reset registers (reset wins over CE per the contract).
  • Equivalence. A2/B2/M track a_flopped/b_flopped/mul_out_reg edge for edge (same stage depth, same clear). sum_ab = a_flopped + b_flopped is the same 17-bit value revision 1 computed in sum_ab_reg, one stage earlier in the logic but at the same cycle index, so inj_reg's content is unchanged from the verified revision. Claim A holds: inj_reg(t)≠0 requires sel(t-1)=1, reset(t-1)=0, which drove RSTP high at that edge, so dsp_p(t)=0 and acc's OR is a select. The induction is sound in both branches (eltwise edge: RSTP clears P, acc={15'b0,sum}; MAC edge: one of p/C is zero, mx is the zero-extended 32-bit product, carries propagate only upward, so acc wraps mod 2^32 exactly as the original's 32-bit qadd). Simultaneous reset+sel and back-to-back eltwise edges both resolve correctly. P[47:32] is never read.
  • Reset/power-up. Mid-run resets (TB reset_pattern, 1/64 random) clear A2/B2/M/P and inj_reg together, matching the original. The block's registers start at 0; inj_reg/a_flopped/b_flopped start unknown but only reach out, which the 4-cycle TB reset (154–156) zeroes before comparison begins.
  • Required items. The single candidate region is listed and converted (none left); blocks added = 84; register stages before/after are given for both the MAC path (4 → 4) and the eltwise path (3 → 3) and both are preserved. No ASSUMES: is needed, and none is claimed.

2815	  end else begin
2816	    add_out_reg <= add_out;
2817	  end
2818	end
2819	
2820	wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] mac_out;
2821	
2822	//down cast the result
2823	assign mac_out = 
2824	    (add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-1] == 0) ?  //positive number
2825	        (
2826	           (|(add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-2 : `DWIDTH-1])) ?  //is any bit from 14:7 is 1, that means overlfow
2827	             {add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-1] , {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b1}}} : //sign bit and then all 1s
2828	             {add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-1] , add_out_reg[`DWIDTH-2:0]} 
2829	        )
2830	        : //negative number
2831	        (
2832	           (|(add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-2 : `DWIDTH-1])) ?  //is any bit from 14:7 is 0, that means overlfow
2833	             {add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-1] , add_out_reg[`DWIDTH-2:0]} :
2834	             {add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-1] , {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}} //sign bit and then all 0s
2835	        );
2836	
2837	
2838	always @(posedge clk) begin
2839	  if (reset) begin
2840	    out <= 0;
2841	  end else begin
2842	    out <= mac_out;
2843	  end
2844	end
2845	
2846	endmodule
2847	
2848	
2849	module qmult(i_multiplicand,i_multiplier,o_result);
2850	input [`DWIDTH-1:0] i_multiplicand;
2851	input [`DWIDTH-1:0] i_multiplier;
2852	output [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] o_result;
2853	
2854	assign o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier;
2855	//DW02_mult #(`DWIDTH,`DWIDTH) u_mult(.A(i_multiplicand), .B(i_multiplier), .TC(1'b1), .PRODUCT(o_result));
2856	
2857	endmodule
2858	
2859	module qadd(a,b,c);
2860	input [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] a;
2861	input [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] b;
2862	output [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] c;
2863	
2864	assign c = a + b;
2865	//DW01_add #(`DWIDTH) u_add(.A(a), .B(b), .CI(1'b0), .SUM(c), .CO());
2866	endmodule

 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/dsp48e2_macc_mreg1.v"

1	// dsp48e2_macc_mreg1: behavioural model of the arch mode dsp48e2_macc_mreg1_mode, which mimics the AMD DSP48E2 slice in the
2	// one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" operation mode (UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12, p46), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
3	// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 1, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY, AMULTSEL = A,
4	// BMULTSEL = B, no pre-adder, no cascade, no pattern detector, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000,
5	// CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
6	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP48E2.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
7	// initial block at lines 1178-1217), read in place and never copied.
8	// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB); M <= A2*B2 if CEM (cleared by RSTM);
9	// P <= f(OPMODE, M, C, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 3 cycles; C and OPMODE to P: 1 cycle
10	// (they are not registered). P wraps at 48 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the eight values below; on
11	// any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X (the mode does not define it).
12	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
13	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
14	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
15	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
16	// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
17	module dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 (
18	    input CLK,
19	    input [26:0] A,
20	    input [17:0] B,
21	    input [47:0] C,
22	    input [8:0] OPMODE,
23	    input RSTA, RSTB, RSTM, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEM, CEP,
24	    output [47:0] P
25	);
26	    reg [26:0] a2 = 0;
27	    reg [17:0] b2 = 0;
28	    reg [47:0] p = 0;
29	    reg signed [44:0] m = 0;                                 // the M register
30	    wire signed [44:0] prod = $signed(a2) * $signed(b2);    // signed 27x18 -> 45, exact
31	    wire [47:0] mx = {{3{m[44]}}, m};
32	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
33	    function [47:0] alu;
34	        input [8:0] opmode;
35	        input [47:0] p;
36	        input [47:0] C;
37	        input [47:0] mx;
38	        case (opmode)
39	            9'b000000101: alu = mx;            // multiply
40	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // multiply-accumulate (Z = P)
41	            9'b000110101: alu = C + mx;        // multiply-add (Z = C)
42	            9'b010110101: alu = p + C + mx;    // multiply-add with P (W = P, Z = C)
43	            9'b110000000: alu = C;             // load C (W = C)
44	            9'b000110000: alu = C;             // load C (Z = C)
45	            9'b010000000: alu = p;             // hold through the ALU (W = P)
46	            9'b000000000: alu = 48'd0;         // zero
47	            default:      alu = {48{1'bx}};    // not a value of this mode
48	        endcase
49	    endfunction
50	    wire [47:0] nxt = alu(OPMODE, p, C, mx);
51	    always @(posedge CLK) begin
52	        if (RSTA) a2 <= 0; else if (CEA2) a2 <= A;
53	        if (RSTB) b2 <= 0; else if (CEB2) b2 <= B;
54	        if (RSTM) m <= 0; else if (CEM) m <= prod;
55	        if (RSTP) p <= 0; else if (CEP) p <= nxt;
56	    end
57	    assign P = p;
58	endmodule

Task

Edit conv_layer.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block

This run offered 2 blocks (dsp48e2_macc and dsp48e2_macc_mreg1). Its first approved plan chose dsp48e2_macc_mreg1, described below, and the choice is fixed for the rest of the run. Instantiate only dsp48e2_macc_mreg1: an edit that instantiates another offered block is refused.

  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, the one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" row of UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12 (p46); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p30). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's; the arch file gives the block one internal delay, not one per internal stage.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTM[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEM[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 v1.11 p38: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). AMD's words end with that sentence; the limits that follow are this project's, derived from it. So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. M loads A2*B2 at a clock edge where CEM is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTM. P loads the value OPMODE selects from M, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. P is always registered; C and OPMODE reach P without an input register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 3 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+2); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= M
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + M
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + M
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + M
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB, RSTM and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2, CEM and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTM, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEM and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at C and OPMODE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Multiply in the approved plan: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339 (module qmult).
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB, RSTM and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2, CEM and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the approved plan calls for.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh conv_layer.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the design source before planning.

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them, one block each): CLK=clk; A={11'b0,a} (27 b, zero-extended, A[26]=0); B={2'b0,b} (18 b, zero-extended, B[17]=0); C={31'b0,inj_reg} (17-bit soft register, see PLAN); OPMODE=9'b010110101, a constant (P <= P + C + M); RSTA=RSTB=RSTM=reset; RSTP=(reset | (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add)); CEA2=CEB2=CEM=CEP=1'b1; P[47:0] to a new wire [47:0] dsp_p. Block registers take over: A2/B2 hold a/b for the multiply, M=mul_out_reg; add_out_reg becomes the pair (P, inj_reg), read as acc = {dsp_p[31:17], dsp_p[16:0] | inj_reg}. a_flopped/b_flopped stay in soft logic as the first stage of the eltwise-add path.

PLAN:

What changes from the kept pass (revision 1), and why. Revision 1 is correct and is the result to beat. Its measured critical path at seed 1 is the register it added: launch flop → three LUT levels of the array's a/b feed (0.660 + 0.452 + 1.494 ns of routing) → $add~1174^ADD~118, the 17-bit {1'b0,a}+{1'b0,b} adder (1.000 wire in, 0.422 carry chain, 0.868 wire out) → the reset-AND LUT → $sdff~1177^Q~15.D, 5.627 ns arrival for a 5.603 ns period. That is sum_ab_reg bit 15. In the unmodified design the same upstream chain ended at a flip-flop setup (a_flopped), about 3.2 ns; revision 1 appended ~2.4 ns of adder and routing to it, and that single path now sets the period at every seed (seed 1 5.603, median 5.342).

This pass moves the adder off the a/b input path: the first stage of the eltwise-add path becomes the design's own a_flopped/b_flopped (16 + 16 soft flip-flops, D driven straight from a/b), and the 17-bit add runs flip-flop-to-flip-flop into inj_reg. Every block input then ends either at a block register (0.019 ns setup: A, B, the four resets, the four clock enables) or, for C, at one local flip-flop ahead of the block's 2.159 ns (inj_reg → C). OPMODE stays a constant, so the high-fanout op broadcast never sees the block's 2.140 ns delay — that was the first pass's 5.4 ns path and must not come back.

Cost, stated plainly: 15 extra soft flip-flops per instance (49 instead of 34), about +1260 BLEs over the design, which I estimate at roughly +7% used area. Break-even against revision 1 is therefore a median period near 5.0 ns; every path I can account for inside seq_mac after this change is at most ~3.9 ns (the inj_reg → C → P path), so the gamble is on what the untouched rest of conv_layer holds, which revision 1's median bounds at ≤5.34 ns.

Design read. DWIDTH=16. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846): a_flopped/b_flopped are a/b registered under the one synchronous reset (2783-2791); qmult forms the 32-bit unsigned product (2793, 2854); mul_out_reg registers it (2795-2801); the muxes on sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add (2807-2808) and qadd (2810, 2864) make the accumulator update exactly
add_out_reg <= reset ? 0 : (sel ? ({16'b0,a_flopped}+{16'b0,b_flopped}) : (add_out_reg + mul_out_reg)) (2812-2818, 32-bit wrap);
mac_out is the saturating down-cast of add_out_reg (2823-2835) and out registers it (2838-2844). Readers checked: a_flopped/b_flopped are read only by the multiply and the sel muxes, mul_out/mul_out_reg only by the adder path, add_out_reg only by the mux and by mac_out; none is a module port, the only output is out. 84 qmult = 84 seq_mac instances (7 matmul_4x4_systolic × 12 live processing_elements, conv_layer.v:1309-1549, 2570-2588, 2648). The module boundary is fixed: tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:42-43 instantiates seq_mac with exactly these ports and drives a, b, eltwise_mode, eltwise_add, reset randomly, so no port may be added, nothing may be hoisted into processing_element, and no assumption about how op/ready_for_eltwise_op sequence the module may be used.

Candidate regions. The one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339, is converted (84 instances). Both operands are 16-bit unsigned: zero-extended they sit at 16 ≤ 26 bits on A and 16 ≤ 17 bits on B, so the top bit of each multiplier operand is 0 and the product is exact. Nothing is left unconverted.

Blocks added. One dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 per seq_mac, 84 in total — the same DSP tile count as the unmodified design (84 of 306).

The rewrite, per seq_mac instance. Replace lines 2775-2835 (keep the port list and the reg [DWIDTH-1:0] out; declaration at 2774, and keep the out` always block at 2838-2844 exactly as it is) with:

verilog
wire sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

// stage 1 of the eltwise-add path: the design's own a_flopped/b_flopped, kept
// in soft logic because A2/B2 inside the block hold the same values but are on
// no block output. D comes straight from a/b, so nothing is added to the
// late-arriving a/b input path.
reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] a_flopped;
reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] b_flopped;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    a_flopped <= 0;
    b_flopped <= 0;
  end else begin
    a_flopped <= a;
    b_flopped <= b;
  end
end

// the 17-bit eltwise sum, now formed register-to-register
wire [`DWIDTH:0] sum_ab = {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};

// stage 2: the eltwise-add result, zero on every other cycle so that C adds
// nothing in MAC mode
reg [`DWIDTH:0] inj_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset | ~sel) inj_reg <= 0;
  else              inj_reg <= sum_ab;
end

wire [47:0] dsp_p;

dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 mac_dsp_u1 (
  .CLK(clk),
  .A({11'b0, a}),
  .B({2'b0, b}),
  .C({31'b0, inj_reg}),
  .OPMODE(9'b010110101),      // constant: P <= P + C + M
  .RSTA(reset), .RSTB(reset), .RSTM(reset),
  .RSTP(reset | sel),         // the eltwise edge clears the accumulator
  .CEA2(1'b1), .CEB2(1'b1), .CEM(1'b1), .CEP(1'b1),
  .P(dsp_p)
);

// the value the original's add_out_reg holds: dsp_p is all-zero on exactly the
// cycles where inj_reg may be non-zero (RSTP cleared P at the previous edge),
// so the OR is a select, not an add.
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = {dsp_p[2*`DWIDTH-1:`DWIDTH+1], (dsp_p[`DWIDTH:0] | inj_reg)};

then keep the down-cast of 2823-2835 verbatim with every add_out_reg replaced by acc, and keep the out register unchanged. qmult/qadd module definitions stay in the file and simply become unused; add_out, add_in1, add_in2, mul_out, mul_out_reg, add_out_reg and the mult_u1/add_u1 instances are deleted. a_flopped/b_flopped and their always block are kept exactly as the original writes them.

Why it matches cycle for cycle. Write S(t) for the original add_out_reg, M₀(t) for mul_out_reg, I(t) for inj_reg, and sel(t), reset(t) for the values present at the edge leaving cycle t.

  • A2/B2/M reproduce a_flopped/b_flopped/mul_out_reg exactly: same loads (clock enables tied 1), same synchronous clear from the one reset (a reset wins over its register's clock enable, as the design's reset wins over its unconditional loads), same power-up value 0.
  • a_flopped/b_flopped in soft logic are the original's registers verbatim, so sum_ab(t) = ({16'b0,a_flopped(t)}+{16'b0,b_flopped(t)})[16:0] on every cycle, including the cycle after any reset (both operands 0 there). The original's 32-bit sum of two zero-extended 16-bit values is exactly {15'b0, sum_ab}.
  • Claim A: I(t) ≠ 0 implies dsp_p(t) = 0. I(t) ≠ 0 needs sel(t-1)=1 and reset(t-1)=0, which drove RSTP high at edge t-1, clearing all 48 bits of P. So acc is a clean select of the two and acc(t)[31:0] is well defined.
  • Induction, acc(t) = S(t). After any reset edge both are 0 (RSTP, and inj_reg's reset), and at power-up the block's registers are 0 as the original's are. At an edge with reset=0 and sel=1: the original takes S <= {15'b0, sum_ab(t)}; the block takes RSTP high so dsp_p(t+1)=0, and I(t+1)=sum_ab(t), so acc(t+1)={15'b0, sum_ab(t)}. At an edge with reset=0 and sel=0: I(t+1)=0, and P loads P + C + M = dsp_p(t) + {31'b0,I(t)} + M(t); by Claim A one of the first two terms is zero, so that 48-bit sum has low 32 bits equal to acc(t)=S(t); M's 48-bit form is {16'b0, product} since both operands are zero-extended and non-negative (the model sign-extends m, whose top bit is 0); carries only propagate upward, so dsp_p(t+1)[31:0] = (S(t)+M₀(t)) mod 2^32 = S(t+1), and acc(t+1)=dsp_p(t+1)[31:0]. Consecutive eltwise edges are covered by the sel=1 case each time, and an eltwise edge followed by MAC edges is covered by the C injection, which puts the exact 17-bit sum back into the accumulator.
  • sel and inj_reg/C are evaluated at the loading edge, as the original's combinational muxes are. OPMODE is a constant legal value, so no edge ever leaves P unknown. P[47:32] may carry accumulator overflow the original drops; it is never read. inj_reg starts unknown, but reset clears it and, before the first reset edge, it reaches only out, which is itself reset; the testbench compares from the first cycle after its 4-cycle reset (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:154-156).

Register stages, operands to result. MAC path, before: a,b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → mul_out_reg (2) → add_out_reg (3) → out (4). After: a,b → A2/B2 (1) → M (2) → P (3) → soft out (4). Eltwise-add path, before: a,b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → add_out_reg (2) → out (3). After: a,b → soft a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → inj_reg, which is the accumulator's visible value for that cycle while P is held at 0 (2) → soft out (3). Both unchanged; the eltwise value additionally enters P one edge later through C, which restores the block's accumulator state before it is next read. Relative to revision 1 the stage count is identical; only the adder's position moved from ahead of stage 1 to between stages 1 and 2.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of seq_mac on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp48e2_macc_mreg1.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

2786	    b_flopped <= 0;

2789	    b_flopped <= b;

2835	        );

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true
===
// dsp48e2_macc_mreg1: behavioural model of the arch mode dsp48e2_macc_mreg1_mode, which mimics the AMD DSP48E2 slice in the
// one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" operation mode (UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12, p46), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 1, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY, AMULTSEL = A,
// BMULTSEL = B, no pre-adder, no cascade, no pattern detector, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000,
// CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP48E2.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
// initial block at lines 1178-1217), read in place and never copied.
// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB); M <= A2*B2 if CEM (cleared by RSTM);
// P <= f(OPMODE, M, C, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 3 cycles; C and OPMODE to P: 1 cycle
// (they are not registered). P wraps at 48 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the eight values below; on
// any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X (the mode does not define it).
// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
modul

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block

This run offered 2 blocks (dsp48e2_macc and dsp48e2_macc_mreg1). Its first approved plan chose dsp48e2_macc_mreg1, described below, and the choice is fixed for the rest of the run. The edit must instantiate only that block.

  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, the one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" row of UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12 (p46); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p30). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's; the arch file gives the block one internal delay, not one per internal stage.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTM[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEM[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 v1.11 p38: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). AMD's words end with that sentence; the limits that follow are this project's, derived from it. So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. M loads A2*B2 at a clock edge where CEM is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTM. P loads the value OPMODE selects from M, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. P is always registered; C and OPMODE reach P without an input register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 3 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+2); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= M
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + M
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + M
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + M
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB, RSTM and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2, CEM and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The file conv_layer.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB, RSTM and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2, CEM and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.

  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The approved plan

I'll read the design source before planning.

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them, one block each): CLK=clk; A={11'b0,a} (27 b, zero-extended, A[26]=0); B={2'b0,b} (18 b, zero-extended, B[17]=0); C={31'b0,inj_reg} (17-bit soft register, see PLAN); OPMODE=9'b010110101, a constant (P <= P + C + M); RSTA=RSTB=RSTM=reset; RSTP=(reset | (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add)); CEA2=CEB2=CEM=CEP=1'b1; P[47:0] to a new wire [47:0] dsp_p. Block registers take over: A2/B2 hold a/b for the multiply, M=mul_out_reg; add_out_reg becomes the pair (P, inj_reg), read as acc = {dsp_p[31:17], dsp_p[16:0] | inj_reg}. a_flopped/b_flopped stay in soft logic as the first stage of the eltwise-add path.

PLAN:

What changes from the kept pass (revision 1), and why. Revision 1 is correct and is the result to beat. Its measured critical path at seed 1 is the register it added: launch flop → three LUT levels of the array's a/b feed (0.660 + 0.452 + 1.494 ns of routing) → $add~1174^ADD~118, the 17-bit {1'b0,a}+{1'b0,b} adder (1.000 wire in, 0.422 carry chain, 0.868 wire out) → the reset-AND LUT → $sdff~1177^Q~15.D, 5.627 ns arrival for a 5.603 ns period. That is sum_ab_reg bit 15. In the unmodified design the same upstream chain ended at a flip-flop setup (a_flopped), about 3.2 ns; revision 1 appended ~2.4 ns of adder and routing to it, and that single path now sets the period at every seed (seed 1 5.603, median 5.342).

This pass moves the adder off the a/b input path: the first stage of the eltwise-add path becomes the design's own a_flopped/b_flopped (16 + 16 soft flip-flops, D driven straight from a/b), and the 17-bit add runs flip-flop-to-flip-flop into inj_reg. Every block input then ends either at a block register (0.019 ns setup: A, B, the four resets, the four clock enables) or, for C, at one local flip-flop ahead of the block's 2.159 ns (inj_reg → C). OPMODE stays a constant, so the high-fanout op broadcast never sees the block's 2.140 ns delay — that was the first pass's 5.4 ns path and must not come back.

Cost, stated plainly: 15 extra soft flip-flops per instance (49 instead of 34), about +1260 BLEs over the design, which I estimate at roughly +7% used area. Break-even against revision 1 is therefore a median period near 5.0 ns; every path I can account for inside seq_mac after this change is at most ~3.9 ns (the inj_reg → C → P path), so the gamble is on what the untouched rest of conv_layer holds, which revision 1's median bounds at ≤5.34 ns.

Design read. DWIDTH=16. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846): a_flopped/b_flopped are a/b registered under the one synchronous reset (2783-2791); qmult forms the 32-bit unsigned product (2793, 2854); mul_out_reg registers it (2795-2801); the muxes on sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add (2807-2808) and qadd (2810, 2864) make the accumulator update exactly
add_out_reg <= reset ? 0 : (sel ? ({16'b0,a_flopped}+{16'b0,b_flopped}) : (add_out_reg + mul_out_reg)) (2812-2818, 32-bit wrap);
mac_out is the saturating down-cast of add_out_reg (2823-2835) and out registers it (2838-2844). Readers checked: a_flopped/b_flopped are read only by the multiply and the sel muxes, mul_out/mul_out_reg only by the adder path, add_out_reg only by the mux and by mac_out; none is a module port, the only output is out. 84 qmult = 84 seq_mac instances (7 matmul_4x4_systolic × 12 live processing_elements, conv_layer.v:1309-1549, 2570-2588, 2648). The module boundary is fixed: tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:42-43 instantiates seq_mac with exactly these ports and drives a, b, eltwise_mode, eltwise_add, reset randomly, so no port may be added, nothing may be hoisted into processing_element, and no assumption about how op/ready_for_eltwise_op sequence the module may be used.

Candidate regions. The one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339, is converted (84 instances). Both operands are 16-bit unsigned: zero-extended they sit at 16 ≤ 26 bits on A and 16 ≤ 17 bits on B, so the top bit of each multiplier operand is 0 and the product is exact. Nothing is left unconverted.

Blocks added. One dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 per seq_mac, 84 in total — the same DSP tile count as the unmodified design (84 of 306).

The rewrite, per seq_mac instance. Replace lines 2775-2835 (keep the port list and the reg [DWIDTH-1:0] out; declaration at 2774, and keep the out` always block at 2838-2844 exactly as it is) with:

verilog
wire sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

// stage 1 of the eltwise-add path: the design's own a_flopped/b_flopped, kept
// in soft logic because A2/B2 inside the block hold the same values but are on
// no block output. D comes straight from a/b, so nothing is added to the
// late-arriving a/b input path.
reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] a_flopped;
reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] b_flopped;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    a_flopped <= 0;
    b_flopped <= 0;
  end else begin
    a_flopped <= a;
    b_flopped <= b;
  end
end

// the 17-bit eltwise sum, now formed register-to-register
wire [`DWIDTH:0] sum_ab = {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};

// stage 2: the eltwise-add result, zero on every other cycle so that C adds
// nothing in MAC mode
reg [`DWIDTH:0] inj_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset | ~sel) inj_reg <= 0;
  else              inj_reg <= sum_ab;
end

wire [47:0] dsp_p;

dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 mac_dsp_u1 (
  .CLK(clk),
  .A({11'b0, a}),
  .B({2'b0, b}),
  .C({31'b0, inj_reg}),
  .OPMODE(9'b010110101),      // constant: P <= P + C + M
  .RSTA(reset), .RSTB(reset), .RSTM(reset),
  .RSTP(reset | sel),         // the eltwise edge clears the accumulator
  .CEA2(1'b1), .CEB2(1'b1), .CEM(1'b1), .CEP(1'b1),
  .P(dsp_p)
);

// the value the original's add_out_reg holds: dsp_p is all-zero on exactly the
// cycles where inj_reg may be non-zero (RSTP cleared P at the previous edge),
// so the OR is a select, not an add.
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = {dsp_p[2*`DWIDTH-1:`DWIDTH+1], (dsp_p[`DWIDTH:0] | inj_reg)};

then keep the down-cast of 2823-2835 verbatim with every add_out_reg replaced by acc, and keep the out register unchanged. qmult/qadd module definitions stay in the file and simply become unused; add_out, add_in1, add_in2, mul_out, mul_out_reg, add_out_reg and the mult_u1/add_u1 instances are deleted. a_flopped/b_flopped and their always block are kept exactly as the original writes them.

Why it matches cycle for cycle. Write S(t) for the original add_out_reg, M₀(t) for mul_out_reg, I(t) for inj_reg, and sel(t), reset(t) for the values present at the edge leaving cycle t.

  • A2/B2/M reproduce a_flopped/b_flopped/mul_out_reg exactly: same loads (clock enables tied 1), same synchronous clear from the one reset (a reset wins over its register's clock enable, as the design's reset wins over its unconditional loads), same power-up value 0.
  • a_flopped/b_flopped in soft logic are the original's registers verbatim, so sum_ab(t) = ({16'b0,a_flopped(t)}+{16'b0,b_flopped(t)})[16:0] on every cycle, including the cycle after any reset (both operands 0 there). The original's 32-bit sum of two zero-extended 16-bit values is exactly {15'b0, sum_ab}.
  • Claim A: I(t) ≠ 0 implies dsp_p(t) = 0. I(t) ≠ 0 needs sel(t-1)=1 and reset(t-1)=0, which drove RSTP high at edge t-1, clearing all 48 bits of P. So acc is a clean select of the two and acc(t)[31:0] is well defined.
  • Induction, acc(t) = S(t). After any reset edge both are 0 (RSTP, and inj_reg's reset), and at power-up the block's registers are 0 as the original's are. At an edge with reset=0 and sel=1: the original takes S <= {15'b0, sum_ab(t)}; the block takes RSTP high so dsp_p(t+1)=0, and I(t+1)=sum_ab(t), so acc(t+1)={15'b0, sum_ab(t)}. At an edge with reset=0 and sel=0: I(t+1)=0, and P loads P + C + M = dsp_p(t) + {31'b0,I(t)} + M(t); by Claim A one of the first two terms is zero, so that 48-bit sum has low 32 bits equal to acc(t)=S(t); M's 48-bit form is {16'b0, product} since both operands are zero-extended and non-negative (the model sign-extends m, whose top bit is 0); carries only propagate upward, so dsp_p(t+1)[31:0] = (S(t)+M₀(t)) mod 2^32 = S(t+1), and acc(t+1)=dsp_p(t+1)[31:0]. Consecutive eltwise edges are covered by the sel=1 case each time, and an eltwise edge followed by MAC edges is covered by the C injection, which puts the exact 17-bit sum back into the accumulator.
  • sel and inj_reg/C are evaluated at the loading edge, as the original's combinational muxes are. OPMODE is a constant legal value, so no edge ever leaves P unknown. P[47:32] may carry accumulator overflow the original drops; it is never read. inj_reg starts unknown, but reset clears it and, before the first reset edge, it reaches only out, which is itself reset; the testbench compares from the first cycle after its 4-cycle reset (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:154-156).

Register stages, operands to result. MAC path, before: a,b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → mul_out_reg (2) → add_out_reg (3) → out (4). After: a,b → A2/B2 (1) → M (2) → P (3) → soft out (4). Eltwise-add path, before: a,b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → add_out_reg (2) → out (3). After: a,b → soft a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → inj_reg, which is the accumulator's visible value for that cycle while P is held at 0 (2) → soft out (3). Both unchanged; the eltwise value additionally enters P one edge later through C, which restores the block's accumulator state before it is next read. Relative to revision 1 the stage count is identical; only the adder's position moved from ahead of stage 1 to between stages 1 and 2.

DESIGN_CHANGE: seq_mac's eltwise-add path adds a_flopped + b_flopped, two values the block keeps in A2/B2 where nothing outside can read them, and it overwrites the accumulator rather than adding to it. Either of two source changes would delete all 49 soft flip-flops this rewrite adds per instance: taking the eltwise addend from a value already outside the multiplier pipeline and applying it on the accumulator (add_in1 staying add_out_reg), so that C alone carries it; or adding the current inputs a + b rather than the flopped pair, which would cut the eltwise path to one stage and let C be driven from a single register.

The edit
--- conv_layer.v (unmodified source)
+++ conv_layer.v (edit under review)
@@ -2772,14 +2772,15 @@
 input clk;

Ports. All 13 inputs connected by contract names at conv_layer.v:2807-2817: CLK, A={11'b0,a} (27 b, A[26]=0), B={2'b0,b} (18 b, B[17]=0), C={31'b0,inj_reg} (48 b), OPMODE=9'b010110101 (a legal value, P <= P+C+M), RSTA/RSTB/RSTM=reset, RSTP=reset|sel, CEA2/CEB2/CEM/CEP=1. P drives dsp_p, read only by acc (2822) → mac_out → out, as the plan says. Unsigned operand widths 16 ≤ 26 on A and 16 ≤ 17 on B, so the product is exact and mx is a zero-extension (m[44]=0).

Behavior. A2/B2/M reproduce a_flopped/b_flopped/mul_out_reg (unconditional load, same synchronous reset, power-up 0). Claim A holds: inj_reg(t)≠0 requires reset=0 ∧ sel=1 at edge t−1, which drove RSTP high, so dsp_p(t)=0 — the OR at 2822 is a select, and acc is well defined. Induction: at sel=1,reset=0 edges RSTP clears P and inj_reg takes sum_ab, giving acc={15'b0,sum_ab}, matching the original's 32-bit zero-extended sum; at sel=0,reset=0 edges P+C has low 32 bits equal to acc(t) in both Claim-A cases, and since carries only propagate upward dsp_p(t+1)[31:0]=(acc(t)+M₀(t)) mod 2^32, matching the original's 32-bit wrap (P[47:32] garbage is never read). Reset edges give 0 on both sides. sel, C and OPMODE are evaluated at the loading edge, as the original's combinational muxes are; OPMODE is constant so no edge leaves P unknown.

Visibility / terms. a_flopped/b_flopped are needed by the eltwise path and are kept in soft logic rather than read out of A2/B2; they are module-local with no other readers. No term dropped; the down-cast at 2827-2839 is the original with add_out_reg→acc (whitespace only otherwise).

Reset / power-up. inj_reg is the one added soft register; the rewrite does not depend on its power-up value — any reset edge clears it, and at a first edge without reset it takes 0 unless sel, in which case it takes the same a_flopped+b_flopped the original depends on. No ASSUMES: line is needed. Simulation: 1156 cycles, 0 mismatches, 0 X, mid-run resets and the reset-between-phases diagnostic both pass.

1	// dsp48e2_macc_mreg1: behavioural model of the arch mode dsp48e2_macc_mreg1_mode, which mimics the AMD DSP48E2 slice in the
2	// one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" operation mode (UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12, p46), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
3	// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 1, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY, AMULTSEL = A,
4	// BMULTSEL = B, no pre-adder, no cascade, no pattern detector, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000,
5	// CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
6	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP48E2.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
7	// initial block at lines 1178-1217), read in place and never copied.
8	// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB); M <= A2*B2 if CEM (cleared by RSTM);
9	// P <= f(OPMODE, M, C, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 3 cycles; C and OPMODE to P: 1 cycle
10	// (they are not registered). P wraps at 48 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the eight values below; on
11	// any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X (the mode does not define it).
12	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
13	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
14	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
15	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
16	// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
17	module dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 (
18	    input CLK,
19	    input [26:0] A,
20	    input [17:0] B,
21	    input [47:0] C,
22	    input [8:0] OPMODE,
23	    input RSTA, RSTB, RSTM, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEM, CEP,
24	    output [47:0] P
25	);
26	    reg [26:0] a2 = 0;
27	    reg [17:0] b2 = 0;
28	    reg [47:0] p = 0;
29	    reg signed [44:0] m = 0;                                 // the M register
30	    wire signed [44:0] prod = $signed(a2) * $signed(b2);    // signed 27x18 -> 45, exact
31	    wire [47:0] mx = {{3{m[44]}}, m};
32	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
33	    function [47:0] alu;
34	        input [8:0] opmode;
35	        input [47:0] p;
36	        input [47:0] C;
37	        input [47:0] mx;
38	        case (opmode)
39	            9'b000000101: alu = mx;            // multiply
40	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // multiply-accumulate (Z = P)
41	            9'b000110101: alu = C + mx;        // multiply-add (Z = C)
42	            9'b010110101: alu = p + C + mx;    // multiply-add with P (W = P, Z = C)
43	            9'b110000000: alu = C;             // load C (W = C)
44	            9'b000110000: alu = C;             // load C (Z = C)
45	            9'b010000000: alu = p;             // hold through the ALU (W = P)
46	            9'b000000000: alu = 48'd0;         // zero
47	            default:      alu = {48{1'bx}};    // not a value of this mode
48	        endcase
49	    endfunction
50	    wire [47:0] nxt = alu(OPMODE, p, C, mx);
51	    always @(posedge CLK) begin
52	        if (RSTA) a2 <= 0; else if (CEA2) a2 <= A;
53	        if (RSTB) b2 <= 0; else if (CEB2) b2 <= B;
54	        if (RSTM) m <= 0; else if (CEM) m <= prod;
55	        if (RSTP) p <= 0; else if (CEP) p <= nxt;
56	    end
57	    assign P = p;
58	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, conv_layer.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block

This run offered 2 blocks (dsp48e2_macc and dsp48e2_macc_mreg1). Your first approved plan chose dsp48e2_macc_mreg1, described below, and the choice is fixed for the rest of the run: every plan from here on uses dsp48e2_macc_mreg1. The header below has no BLOCK: line; do not write one, even where an earlier plan shown here has it.

  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc_mreg1 (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, the one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" row of UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12 (p46); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p30). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's; the arch file gives the block one internal delay, not one per internal stage.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTM[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEM[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 v1.11 p38: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). AMD's words end with that sentence; the limits that follow are this project's, derived from it. So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. M loads A2*B2 at a clock edge where CEM is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTM. P loads the value OPMODE selects from M, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. P is always registered; C and OPMODE reach P without an input register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 3 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+2); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= M
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + M
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + M
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + M
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB, RSTM and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2, CEM and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.

Candidate regions

Multiplies in the unmodified conv_layer, counted in its packed netlist at seed 1: 84 on 84 DSP tiles, all in two_mult_18x19 mode (1 to a tile). The DSP tile count is the same at each of the five seeds. Multiplies in soft logic, if any, are not in this count.

Each row is one multiply of the design as elaborated from the source. An operand's bits are the bits it carries, the zero or sign extension to the width of the expression left out; signed or unsigned is how the multiply reads it. Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB, RSTM and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2, CEM and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.
